I mean, I’ve had days where I’m frustrated or worn out, especially when I’m messing up things at work that I normally keep straight, and then I get kind of hard on myself about it.\n\nBut if you mean like… am I feeling low all the time, or like everything is hopeless, I’m not sure I’d say that. I haven’t really been thinking of myself as depressed. It’s more that I’m bothered and worried because I don’t feel as sharp or steady as I’m used to feeling. Some days that weighs on me, sure, but I don’t know if I’d call it depression exactly.\n\nDoctor: Did these concentration problems start after a major stress or upsetting event in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, there was a life change around when it started, I guess. I don’t know if I’d call it one big upsetting event exactly, but my daughter moved back home, and that shifted a lot in the house.\n\nIt’s been about seven months now, roughly, since all this started getting noticeable. And around that time I was just trying to keep everything steady — work, home, making sure things didn’t feel chaotic, trying not to make a big deal out of it. I think I kept telling myself I could just manage it, you know, keep the routine going and stay on top of things like I always have.\n\nBut looking back, that’s about when my concentration started slipping more. Not overnight, like a switch flipped, but that’s the time frame I keep coming back to — since my daughter came back home and I was trying to hold everything together.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot more than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’m worrying a lot more than usual, exactly. I mean, I worry some, sure — I think anybody would when things feel a little off or when you’re trying to keep up with responsibilities — but I haven’t really noticed myself just sitting around worrying nonstop or feeling like I can’t shut my mind off.\n\nIt’s more like I get bothered by things in the moment, and then I think about them maybe longer than I should, but I don’t know if that’s “more than usual” for me. I’ve always been the kind of person who tries to stay on top of things, so it’s hard to separate normal concern from actual worrying, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been forgetting recent conversations or appointments?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like I completely forget the whole conversation happened, but I miss pieces, and that’s what’s been bothering me. Like, someone will tell me a detail — “drop this at the side entrance,” or “call me back after lunch,” or there’ll be a note from a client about a delivery — and I swear I hear it at the time, but then later it’s like that one part didn’t stick.\n\nAnd it’s not just appointments, exactly. It’s more that my attention gets pulled all over the place. I’ll be driving and the CB chirps, or I see a fuel price sign, or some new thought pops into my head about a route or something I should do, and then I lose track of the thing I was supposed to remember. Then I’m standing there later going, wait, who did I promise to call back? What was the specific instruction they gave me?\n\nThat’s really what finally got me in here. I’m used to being pretty solid with those details. My work depends on it, you know? And over these months, I’ve been missing little things I normally never would, and it makes me feel like my mind keeps jumping before I can pin things down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble finding common words when you speak?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, everybody blanks on a word once in a while, like you’re tired or distracted and you say “that thing” instead of the name of it, but I haven’t noticed that being a real problem for me.\n\nWhen I’m talking, I can usually say what I mean. I might lose track of what I was going to say if there’s too much going on, but it’s not like I’m sitting there unable to find normal words or calling things by the wrong name. At least not that I’ve picked up on, and nobody’s pointed it out to me either.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone close to you noticed that your memory or concentration has changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a couple people have noticed, mostly because it shows up in practical ways. It’s not like they’re saying, “You don’t remember who I am,” or anything big like that. It’s more my wife will say, “I told you that already,” or someone at work will remind me, “You said you were going to call him back,” and I’ll realize I lost that thread somewhere.\n\nWhat finally got me to come in was that, over these months, my mind just keeps jumping around. Like I’ll hear the CB chirp, or I’ll notice a fuel price sign, or I’ll suddenly think of some route change or something I need to handle, and then whatever detail I was supposed to hold onto just gets knocked loose. A client might have given me a delivery note, or someone told me exactly who to call back and when, and later I’m piecing it together like, wait, what was that again?\n\nPeople close to me don’t act like I’m completely forgetting everything, but they have noticed I’m not as locked in as I usually am. And honestly, I’ve noticed it too, because I’m normally pretty good with those little details. That’s part of the job. So when I start missing things I normally wouldn’t miss, other people catch it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent head injury?\n\nPatient: No, not that I can think of. I mean, I drive for work, so I’ve had the usual bumps and jolts over the years, climbing in and out of trucks, hitting rough roads, that kind of thing, but nothing recent where I hit my head hard or got knocked out or anything like that.\n\nI’ve been trying to think back because I know that can matter, but I don’t remember any accident or fall lately where I’d say, “Yeah, that’s when this started.” Nothing like that stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: For the past several months, have you felt down or sad most days?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “down or sad most days,” exactly. I mean, I’ve had days where I’m frustrated or worn out, sure, especially when I’m not feeling like myself, but it’s not like I wake up every morning feeling sad and it stays there all day.\n\nIt’s more hard for me to pin down. If you asked my wife, she might say I’ve been more off or bothered by things, but sad… I’m not sure that’s the right word. I haven’t really been thinking of it as being depressed or anything. More like I get irritated with myself when I’m not keeping up the way I normally do, and then that can put me in a bad mood. But most days sad? No, I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it excessive worrying about a lot of different things, no. I mean, I worry about the normal stuff, I guess — work, money, keeping up with things, whether I missed something I was supposed to do — but I don’t know that it feels like I’m sitting around worrying all day about every possible thing.\n\nSometimes if something’s right in front of me, I’ll chew on it for a while, especially if I’m not sure I handled it right. But it’s not like I’m constantly thinking, “What if this happens, what if that happens,” across every part of my life. At least I don’t think so.\n\nI guess I’d say I get concerned or irritated when something needs my attention, but I wouldn’t describe myself as a huge worrier in general.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in a clear way. I mean, I still like the things I usually like when I actually get around to them. It’s not like food tastes like cardboard or I can’t enjoy watching something on TV or talking with somebody I like.\n\nSometimes I don’t feel as pulled toward doing things, but I don’t know if that’s “lost interest” exactly. Some days I’m just kind of caught up in whatever’s going on and I don’t make time for stuff. But if I sit down and do it, I can still get something out of it.\n\nSo… I guess no, I haven’t really noticed that I’ve stopped enjoying things. At least not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you been drinking more alcohol than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I drink sometimes, but I haven’t really been keeping a chart or anything, so it’s hard for me to say if it’s “more than usual” in a precise way.\n\nIf you mean like drinking every night or getting drunk more often, no, I don’t think that’s been happening. I might have a beer here and there, or if there’s something going on, but it hasn’t stood out to me like, “Wow, I’m drinking a lot lately.” My wife hasn’t said anything about it either, and she usually notices that kind of stuff before I do.\n\nSo… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble keeping your balance?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’ve had trouble keeping my balance in any way that’s stood out to me.\n\nLike, I’m not bumping into walls or feeling like I’m going to fall over or anything. Sometimes if I stand up too fast I might have to get my bearings for a second, but I think that’s pretty normal, and it’s not something I’ve been worried about. I’m on my feet enough during the day that I feel like I’d probably notice if I was unsteady.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t really noticed balance problems.\n\nDoctor: Have you started having trouble managing everyday tasks that used to be easy for you, like paying bills or handling your medications?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not so much like I suddenly don’t know how to pay a bill or I can’t remember how to do basic stuff, but I have noticed things slipping more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAt work, especially, I’ve had dispatch and sometimes clients repeat details to me that I really should’ve kept straight the first time. Like addresses, timing, little instructions — things I normally pride myself on tracking because that’s part of the job. And then I’ll realize I was already thinking about three other things I wanted to get done, or some idea I was chasing, and I didn’t really lock in on what they said. A couple of sales follow-ups slipped through that way too, which bothered me because that’s not like me. I’ll have all these things I’m trying to move on at once, and then one of the normal, boring-but-important steps gets missed.\n\nAt home… it’s more that I can tell I’m making things harder on my wife and daughter. They’ve been kind of walking on eggshells around me, and I hate saying that because I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming them. It’s me adding strain when I’m supposed to be helping keep things steady. So, yeah, everyday tasks aren’t impossible or anything, but I’m not managing them as smoothly as I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energized or irritable for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I guess that’s probably one of the bigger things, now that you say it that way.\n\nIt’s not like just waking up in a good mood for a day. For months now I’ve had these stretches where I’m way more fired up than I usually am, like I’ve got this feeling that everything is going to work out and I can push through anything, like problems aren’t really problems. And in the moment it can feel good, honestly, because I feel confident and kind of unstoppable in a way.\n\nBut then there’s the other side of it, where I’m irritable over stupid little things. Especially at home or when I’m out in the yard doing something, I’ll snap about stuff that normally wouldn’t bother me much. Like something being out of place, or something not going the way I had it in my head, and suddenly I’m reacting bigger than the situation deserves. That’s not really how I think of myself, or at least not how I want to be.\n\nAnd it’s been more than a couple days here and there. It’s been going on for months, where I’m either too keyed up and convinced everything’s fine, or I’m getting sharp with people over small stuff. So… yeah, several days in a row, and honestly longer than that sometimes.\n\nDoctor: During those keyed-up periods, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually been pretty noticeable.\n\nMost nights lately I’m only sleeping maybe three or four hours, something like that, and then I’m just… up. Like before daylight a lot of the time. And the weird part is I don’t wake up thinking, “Oh great, I’m exhausted, I need more sleep.” I’ll wake up and feel like I’m ready to roll, like I should just get moving and start doing things.\n\nIt doesn’t feel normal when I say it out loud, because I know three or four hours isn’t much. But in the moment I don’t feel worn out the way I’d expect to. I’ll be lying there awake and then it’s like, well, I might as well get up. So, yeah — during those times, I definitely don’t seem to need as much sleep as usual.\n\nDoctor: During these keyed-up times, have you done anything so out of character or risky that it caused serious problems for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “serious problems,” at least not the way I’m hearing that.\n\nI mean, I’ve definitely been more wound up and maybe quicker to jump into things than I normally would, and I know that can spill over in ways that aren’t great. So… yeah, several days in a row, and honestly longer than that sometimes.\n\nDoctor: During those keyed-up periods, did you need less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yeah. That’s actually been pretty noticeable.\n\nMost nights lately I’m only sleeping maybe three or four hours, something like that, and then I’m just… up. Like before daylight a lot of the time. And the weird part is I don’t wake up thinking, “Oh great, I’m exhausted, I need more sleep.” I’ll wake up and feel like I’m ready to roll, like I should just get moving and start doing things.\n\nIt doesn’t feel normal when I say it out loud, because I know three or four hours isn’t much. But in the moment I don’t feel worn out the way I’d expect to. I’ll be lying there awake and then it’s like, well, I might as well get up. So, yeah — during those times, I definitely don’t seem to need as much sleep as usual.\n\nDoctor: During these keyed-up times, have you done anything so out of character or risky that it caused serious problems for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not “serious problems,” at least not the way I’m hearing that.\n\nI mean, I’ve definitely been more wound up and maybe quicker to jump into things than I normally would, and I know that can spill over in ways that aren’t great. But if you mean like doing something totally reckless or way outside who I am that blew up my life or got me in real trouble, I can’t really point to something like that.\n\nPart of me hesitates because I don’t want to downplay it if my family would describe it differently, you know? Sometimes when you’re in it, you don’t see how intense you’re coming across. But as far as something clearly risky that caused serious consequences… no, I don’t have an example of that."
